Provides strategy-based cleaning operations including deduplication, missing value imputation, text processing, and outlier handling through a configurable pipeline.\n\n## Core Components\n\n### CleaningStrategies\nCollection of static methods for specific cleaning operations:\n\n#### Deduplication\n- `remove_duplicates(df, subset=None)` - Remove duplicate rows given the context of the prompt or the context of the dataset, Carefully evaluate if it still makes sense to allow duplicates exist in the downstream analysis. You should always check the data and remove them based on the logic of meta data and downstream goal.\n\n#### Missing Value Handling\nLogic: If the column is not critical to affect the dowstream task and the cost of dropping the whole line is not neccessary, you should try to impute them, otherwise you can drop.\n- `drop_missing(df, columns)` - Drop rows with missing critical values (that will affect the downstream task's peformance) in specified columns.\n- `impute_median(df, columns)` - Impute with median for numerical columns if the column is numerical data\n- `impute_knn(df, target_features, n_neighbors=5)` - KNN-based imputation such that the target column value potentially can be inferred by other columns. I.e. you have to carefully see the semantic and logical relationship between columns in terms of real life cases and make decisions to use this method for better data imputation performance.\n- `impute_mode(df, columns)` - Impute with mode for categorical columns if the target column is relatively independent to other columns and should just be filled with an acceptable value.\n\n#### Text Processing\n- `process_text(df, columns, operation)` - Text operations: some columns might need conduct text related processing to get numbers or remove unnecessary content, you need to dertermine if this is needed based on preview the column values regarding the down stream goal. (extract_numbers, clean_whitespace, extract_email, lowercase, remove_special)\n\n#### Outlier Handling\nLogic: Again you need to make sure if removing outliers is costly or not in terms of the sample size or critical columns under your investigation. Keep the rows by replacing the outlier value with a more reasonable value, or simply remove the whole row in one of the two ways if it barely has any impact.\n- `cap_outliers_iqr(df, columns, multiplier=1.5)` - Winsorization using IQR to keep reasonable signals of the data;\n- `remove_outliers_iqr(df, columns, multiplier=1.5)` - Remove outliers using IQR\n- `remove_outliers_zscore(df, columns, threshold=3.0)` - Remove outliers using Z-score\n\n### DataCleaningPipeline\nOrchestrates multiple cleaning steps with logging and progress tracking.\n\n## Usage\n\n```python\nfrom data_cleaning import CleaningStrategies, DataCleaningPipeline\n\n# Create pipeline\npipeline = DataCleaningPipeline(name=\"Transaction Data\")\n\n# Add cleaning steps\npipeline.add_step(\n    CleaningStrategies.remove_duplicates,\n    subset=['customer_id', 'order_date'],\n    description=\"Remove duplicate orders\"\n).add_step(\n    CleaningStrategies.drop_missing,\n    columns=['customer_id', 'amount'],\n    description=\"Drop rows with missing critical fields\"\n).add_step(\n    CleaningStrategies.impute_knn,\n    target_features={\n        'income': {'features': ['age', 'education'], 'type': 'numeric'}\n    },\n    description=\"KNN imputation for income\"\n).add_step(\n    CleaningStrategies.cap_outliers_iqr,\n    columns=['amount'],\n    multiplier=1.5,\n    description=\"Cap price outliers\"\n)\n\n# Execute pipeline\ndf_clean = pipeline.execute(raw_df, verbose=True)\n\n# Get execution log\nlog_df = pipeline.get_log()\n```\n\n## Input\n- Any tabular DataFrame requiring cleaning\n\n## Output\n- Cleaned DataFrame with execution log available\n\n## Best Practices\n- Chain multiple steps using method chaining\n- Use verbose=True to monitor pipeline progress\n- Review execution log for quality assurance\n- Extend CleaningStrategies with domain-specific methods as needed\n\n\nARGUMENTS: /app/data/survey_dirty.csv /app/data/amazon-purchases-2019-2020_dirty.csv"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"d971beb3-b558-4922-a5ce-2e0c3ea4024d","uuid":"a1a55ba6-c74f-4f89-855e-657d28748467","isSynthetic":true}

Prepare data for Prophet\n        df_prophet = category_data[['Order Date', 'Total_Spend']].copy()\n        df_prophet.columns = ['ds', 'y']\n        df_prophet = df_prophet.sort_values('ds')\n\n        # Need at least some historical data\n        if len(df_prophet) < 30:\n            return None\n\n        # Use the framework to detect anomalies\n        framework = AnomalyDetectionFramework(\n            seasonality_mode='additive',\n            yearly_seasonality=False,\n            weekly_seasonality=True,\n            daily_seasonality=False\n        )\n\n        results = framework.detect_anomalies(\n            df_prophet,\n            forecast_periods=0,  # No need to forecast beyond the data\n            interval_width=0.95\n        )\n\n        return results\n\n    except Exception as e:\n        return None\n\n\ndef calculate_anomaly_index(daily_sales_df):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate anomaly index for each category\n    Comparing March 2020 (treatment) to Jan-Feb 2020 (baseline)\n    Index ranges from -100 (slump) to +100 (surge)\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\"*80)\n    print(\"CALCULATING ANOMALY INDEX BY CATEGORY\")\n    print(\"=\"*80)\n\n    baseline_start = pd.Timestamp('2020-01-01')\n    baseline_end = pd.Timestamp('2020-02-29')\n    treatment_start = pd.Timestamp('2020-03-01')\n    treatment_end = pd.Timestamp('2020-03-31')\n\n    # Historical data for Prophet training (all data before treatment)\n    historical_end = pd.Timestamp('2019-12-31')\n\n    anomaly_results = []\n\n    categories = daily_sales_df['Category'].unique()\n    print(f\"Processing {len(categories)} categories...\")\n\n    for idx, category in enumerate(categories):\n        if idx % 50 == 0:\n            print(f\"  Processed {idx}/{len(categories)} categories...\")\n\n        cat_data = daily_sales_df[daily_sales_df['Category'] == category].copy()\n        cat_data = cat_data.sort_values('Order Date')\n\n        # Get baseline data (Jan-Feb 2020)\n        baseline_data = cat_data[\n            (cat_data['Order Date'] >= baseline_start) &\n            (cat_data['Order Date'] <= baseline_end)\n        ]\n\n        # Get treatment data (Mar 2020)\n        treatment_data = cat_data[\n            (cat_data['Order Date'] >= treatment_start) &\n            (cat_data['Order Date'] <= treatment_end)\n        ]\n\n        # Need both periods to have data\n        if len(baseline_data) == 0 or len(treatment_data) == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Calculate statistics\n        baseline_avg = baseline_data['Total_Spend'].mean()\n        treatment_avg = treatment_data['Total_Spend'].mean()\n\n        # Avoid division by zero\n        if baseline_avg == 0:\n            baseline_avg = 1e-6\n\n        # Calculate percentage change\n        pct_change = ((treatment_avg - baseline_avg) / baseline_avg) * 100\n\n        # Normalize to -100 to +100 scale\n        # Use logistic-like transformation to bound the values\n        # Larger positive changes -> higher index (up to ~100)\n        # Larger negative changes -> lower index (down to ~-100)\n        anomaly_index = np.tanh(pct_change / 50) * 100\n\n        # Additional signal from volatility\n        # High volatility in treatment period suggests instability\n        treatment_std = treatment_data['Total_Spend'].std()\n        baseline_std = baseline_data['Total_Spend'].std()\n\n        if baseline_std > 0:\n            volatility_ratio = treatment_std / baseline_std\n        else:\n            volatility_ratio = 1\n\n        # Adjust anomaly index based on volatility\n        # Increased volatility suggests more \"anomalous\" behavior\n        volatility_adjustment = min((volatility_ratio - 1) * 10, 20)  # Cap adjustment\n        anomaly_index = np.clip(anomaly_index + volatility_adjustment, -100, 100)\n\n        anomaly_results.append({\n            'Category': category,\n            'Anomaly_Index': anomaly_index,\n            'Baseline_Avg_Spend': baseline_avg,\n            'Treatment_Avg_Spend': treatment_avg,\n            'Percent_Change': pct_change,\n            'Baseline_Count': len(baseline_data),\n            'Treatment_Count': len(treatment_data),\n            'Baseline_Std': baseline_std,\n            'Treatment_Std': treatment_std,\n            'Volatility_Ratio': volatility_ratio\n        })\n\n    # Create results dataframe and sort by anomaly index\n    anomaly_df = pd.DataFrame(anomaly_results)\n    anomaly_df = anomaly_df.sort_values('Anomaly_Index', ascending=False)\n\n    print(f\"\\nProcessed {len(anomaly_df)} categories with data in both periods\")\n\n    # Save full results\n    anomaly_df.to_csv('/app/output/category_anomaly_index.csv', index=False)\n    print(f\"Saved anomaly index to: /app/output/category_anomaly_index.csv\")\n\n    # Print summary statistics\n    print(\"\\nAnomaly Index Summary Statistics:\")\n    print(f\"  Mean: {anomaly_df['Anomaly_Index'].mean():.2f}\")\n    print(f\"  Std Dev: {anomaly_df['Anomaly_Index'].std():.2f}\")\n    print(f\"  Min: {anomaly_df['Anomaly_Index'].min():.2f}\")\n    print(f\"  Max: {anomaly_df['Anomaly_Index'].max():.2f}\")\n\n    # Top 10 surges (highest anomaly index)\n    print(\"\\nTop 10 SURGE Categories (Anomaly Index):\")\n    top_surges = anomaly_df.head(10)\n    for i, row in top_surges.iterrows():\n        print(f\"  {row['Category']}: {row['Anomaly_Index']:.2f} \" +\n              f\"(Baseline: ${row['Baseline_Avg_Spend']:.2f}, \" +\n              f\"Treatment: ${row['Treatment_Avg_Spend']:.2f}, \" +\n              f\"Change: {row['Percent_Change']:.1f}%)\")\n\n    # Top 10 slumps (lowest anomaly index)\n    print(\"\\nTop 10 SLUMP Categories (Anomaly Index):\")\n    top_slumps = anomaly_df.tail(10)\n    for i, row in top_slumps.iloc[::-1].iterrows():\n        print(f\"  {row['Category']}: {row['Anomaly_Index']:.2f} \" +\n              f\"(Baseline: ${row['Baseline_Avg_Spend']:.2f}, \" +\n              f\"Treatment: ${row['Treatment_Avg_Spend']:.2f}, \" +\n              f\"Change: {row['Percent_Change']:.1f}%)\")\n\n    return anomaly_df\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    # Ensure Order Date is datetime\n    purchases_df['Order Date'] = pd.to_datetime(purchases_df['Order Date'])\n\n    # Aggregate daily spending by category\n    daily_sales = purchases_df.groupby(['Order Date', 'Category']).agg({\n        'Total_Spend': 'sum',\n        'Survey ResponseID': 'count'\n    }).reset_index()\n    daily_sales.rename(columns={'Survey ResponseID': 'Transaction_Count'}, inplace=True)\n\n    print(f\"Created {len(daily_sales)} daily category observations\")\n    print(f\"Date range: {daily_sales['Order Date'].min()} to {daily_sales['Order Date'].max()}\")\n    print(f\"Unique categories: {daily_sales['Category'].nunique()}\")\n\n    return daily_sales\n\n\ndef calculate_anomaly_index(daily_sales_df):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate anomaly index for each category\n    Comparing March 2020 (treatment) to Jan-Feb 2020 (baseline)\n    Index ranges from -100 (slump) to +100 (surge)\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\"*80)\n    print(\"CALCULATING ANOMALY INDEX BY CATEGORY\")\n    print(\"=\"*80)\n\n    baseline_start = pd.Timestamp('2020-01-01')\n    baseline_end = pd.Timestamp('2020-02-29')\n    treatment_start = pd.Timestamp('2020-03-01')\n    treatment_end = pd.Timestamp('2020-03-31')\n\n    anomaly_results = []\n    categories = daily_sales_df['Category'].unique()\n    print(f\"Processing {len(categories)} categories...\")\n\n    for idx, category in enumerate(categories):\n        if idx % 50 == 0:\n            print(f\"  Processed {idx}/{len(categories)} categories...\")\n\n        cat_data = daily_sales_df[daily_sales_df['Category'] == category].copy()\n        cat_data = cat_data.sort_values('Order Date')\n\n        # Get baseline data (Jan-Feb 2020)\n        baseline_data = cat_data[\n            (cat_data['Order Date'] >= baseline_start) &\n            (cat_data['Order Date'] <= baseline_end)\n        ]\n\n        # Get treatment data (Mar 2020)\n        treatment_data = cat_data[\n            (cat_data['Order Date'] >= treatment_start) &\n            (cat_data['Order Date'] <= treatment_end)\n        ]\n\n        # Need both periods to have data\n        if len(baseline_data) == 0 or len(treatment_data) == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Calculate statistics\n        baseline_avg = baseline_data['Total_Spend'].mean()\n        treatment_avg = treatment_data['Total_Spend'].mean()\n\n        # Avoid division by zero\n        if baseline_avg == 0:\n            baseline_avg = 1e-6\n\n        # Calculate percentage change\n        pct_change = ((treatment_avg - baseline_avg) / baseline_avg) * 100\n\n        # Normalize to -100 to +100 scale using hyperbolic tangent\n        # This bounds the value while allowing large changes to approach ±100\n        anomaly_index = np.tanh(pct_change / 50) * 100\n\n        # Additional signal from volatility\n        # High volatility in treatment period suggests instability\n        treatment_std = treatment_data['Total_Spend'].std()\n        baseline_std = baseline_data['Total_Spend'].std()\n\n        if baseline_std > 0:\n            volatility_ratio = treatment_std / baseline_std\n        else:\n            volatility_ratio = 1\n\n        # Adjust anomaly index based on volatility\n        # Increased volatility suggests more \"anomalous\" behavior\n        volatility_adjustment = min((volatility_ratio - 1) * 10, 20)  # Cap adjustment\n        anomaly_index = np.clip(anomaly_index + volatility_adjustment, -100, 100)\n\n        anomaly_results.append({\n            'Category': category,\n            'Anomaly_Index': anomaly_index,\n            'Baseline_Avg_Spend': baseline_avg,\n            'Treatment_Avg_Spend': treatment_avg,\n            'Percent_Change': pct_change,\n            'Baseline_Count': len(baseline_data),\n            'Treatment_Count': len(treatment_data),\n            'Baseline_Std': baseline_std,\n            'Treatment_Std': treatment_std,\n            'Volatility_Ratio': volatility_ratio\n        })\n\n    # Top 10 surges (highest anomaly index)\n    print(\"\\nTop 10 SURGE Categories (Anomaly Index):\")\n    top_surges = anomaly_df.head(10)\n    for i, row in top_surges.iterrows():\n        print(f\"  {row['Category']}: {row['Anomaly_Index']:.2f} \" +\n              f\"(Baseline: ${row['Baseline_Avg_Spend']:.2f}, \" +\n              f\"Treatment: ${row['Treatment_Avg_Spend']:.2f}, \" +\n              f\"Change: {row['Percent_Change']:.1f}%)\")\n\n    # Need both periods to have data\n        if len(baseline_data) == 0 or len(treatment_data) == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Calculate statistics\n        baseline_avg = baseline_data['Total_Spend'].mean()\n        treatment_avg = treatment_data['Total_Spend'].mean()\n\n        # Additional signal from volatility\n        # High volatility in treatment period suggests instability\n        treatment_std = treatment_data['Total_Spend'].std()\n        baseline_std = baseline_data['Total_Spend'].std()\n\n        if baseline_std > 0:\n            volatility_ratio = treatment_std / baseline_std\n        else:\n            volatility_ratio = 1\n\n        # Adjust anomaly index based on volatility\n        # Increased volatility suggests more \"anomalous\" behavior\n        volatility_adjustment = min((volatility_ratio - 1) * 10, 20)  # Cap adjustment\n        anomaly_index = np.clip(anomaly_index + volatility_adjustment, -100, 100)\n\n        anomaly_results.append({\n            'Category': category,\n            'Anomaly_Index': anomaly_index,\n            'Baseline_Avg_Spend': baseline_avg,\n            'Treatment_Avg_Spend': treatment_avg,\n            'Percent_Change': pct_change,\n            'Baseline_Count': len(baseline_data),\n            'Treatment_Count': len(treatment_data),\n            'Baseline_Std': baseline_std,\n            'Treatment_Std': treatment_std,\n            'Volatility_Ratio': volatility_ratio\n        })\n\n    # Create results dataframe and sort by anomaly index\n    anomaly_df = pd.DataFrame(anomaly_results)\n    anomaly_df = anomaly_df.sort_values('Anomaly_Index', ascending=False)\n\n    print(f\"\\nProcessed {len(anomaly_df)} categories with data in both periods\")\n\n    # Save full results\n    anomaly_df.to_csv('/app/output/category_anomaly_index.csv', index=False)\n    print(f\"Saved anomaly index to: /app/output/category_anomaly_index.csv\")\n\n    # Print summary statistics\n    print(\"\\nAnomaly Index Summary Statistics:\")\n    print(f\"  Mean: {anomaly_df['Anomaly_Index'].mean():.2f}\")\n    print(f\"  Std Dev: {anomaly_df['Anomaly_Index'].std():.2f}\")\n    print(f\"  Min: {anomaly_df['Anomaly_Index'].min():.2f}\")\n    print(f\"  Max: {anomaly_df['Anomaly_Index'].max():.2f}\")\n\n    # Top 10 surges (highest anomaly index)\n    print(\"\\nTop 10 SURGE Categories (Anomaly Index):\")\n    top_surges = anomaly_df.head(10)\n    for i, row in top_surges.iterrows():\n        print(f\"  {row['Category']}: {row['Anomaly_Index']:.2f} \" +\n              f\"(Baseline: ${row['Baseline_Avg_Spend']:.2f}, \" +\n              f\"Treatment: ${row['Treatment_Avg_Spend']:.2f}, \" +\n              f\"Change: {row['Percent_Change']:.1f}%)\")\n\n    # Top 10 slumps (lowest anomaly index)\n    print(\"\\nTop 10 SLUMP Categories (Anomaly Index):\")\n    top_slumps = anomaly_df.tail(10)\n    for i, row in top_slumps.iloc[::-1].iterrows():\n        print(f\"  {row['Category']}: {row['Anomaly_Index']:.2f} \" +\n              f\"(Baseline: ${row['Baseline_Avg_Spend']:.2f}, \" +\n              f\"Treatment: ${row['Treatment_Avg_Spend']:.2f}, \" +\n              f\"Change: {row['Percent_Change']:.1f}%)\")\n\n    return anomaly_df\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    # Load cleaned data\n    purchases_df = pd.read_csv('/app/output/amazon-purchases-2019-2020-filtered.csv')\n    engineered_features_df = pd.read_csv('/app/output/survey_feature_engineered.csv')\n    anomaly_index_df = pd.read_csv('/app/output/category_anomaly_index.csv')\n\n    # Convert date\n    purchases_df['Order Date'] = pd.to_datetime(purchases_df['Order Date'])\n\n    # Define periods\n    baseline_start = pd.Timestamp('2020-01-01')\n    baseline_end = pd.Timestamp('2020-02-29')\n    treatment_start = pd.Timestamp('2020-03-01')\n    treatment_end = pd.Timestamp('2020-03-31')\n\n    # Identify top 10 surge and top 10 slump categories\n    top_surges = anomaly_index_df.nlargest(10, 'Anomaly_Index')['Category'].tolist()\n    top_slumps = anomaly_index_df.nsmallest(10, 'Anomaly_Index')['Category'].tolist()\n\n    print(f\"\\nTop 10 Surge Categories: {top_surges}\")\n    print(f\"Top 10 Slump Categories: {top_slumps}\")\n\n    # Keep only treatment and top categories\n    treatment_categories = top_surges + top_slumps\n    purchases_df = purchases_df[purchases_df['Category'].isin(treatment_categories)].copy()\n\n    # Create period indicator\n    def assign_period(date):\n        if baseline_start <= date <= baseline_end:\n            return 'Baseline'\n        elif treatment_start <= date <= treatment_end:\n            return 'Treatment'\n        else:\n            return None\n\n    purchases_df['Period'] = purchases_df['Order Date'].apply(assign_period)\n    purchases_df = purchases_df[purchases_df['Period'].notna()].copy()\n\n    print(f\"\\nTotal transactions in baseline and treatment periods: {len(purchases_df)}\")\n\n    # ==========================================\n    # INTENSIVE MARGIN DATA (Spending Amount)\n    # ==========================================\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"-\"*80)\n    print(\"PREPARING INTENSIVE MARGIN DATA (Spending Amount)\")\n    print(\"-\"*80)\n\n    # Aggregate spending by user-category-period\n    intensive_data = purchases_df.groupby(\n        ['Survey ResponseID', 'Category', 'Period']\n    ).agg({\n        'Total_Spend': 'sum',\n        'Order Date': 'count'  # Transaction count\n    }).reset_index()\n    intensive_data.rename(columns={'Order Date': 'Transaction_Count'}, inplace=True)\n    intensive_data.rename(columns={'Total_Spend': 'Total_Spend'}, inplace=True)\n\n    # Select relevant columns for output\n    output_cols = ['Survey ResponseID', 'Category', 'Period', 'Total_Spend']\n    # Add engineered features\n    engineered_cols = [col for col in intensive_data.columns if col.startswith('ENG_')]\n    output_cols.extend(engineered_cols)\n    output_cols.append('Anomaly_Index')\n\n    intensive_output = intensive_data[output_cols].copy()\n    intensive_output.to_csv(\n        '/app/output/user_category_period_aggregated_intensive.csv',\n        index=False\n    )\n\n    print(f\"Saved intensive margin data to: /app/output/user_category_period_aggregated_intensive.csv\")\n    print(f\"  - Rows: {len(intensive_output)}\")\n    print(f\"  - Columns: {len(output_cols)}\")\n    print(f\"  - Unique users: {intensive_output['Survey ResponseID'].nunique()}\")\n    print(f\"  - Unique categories: {intensive_output['Category'].nunique()}\")\n\n    # Create purchase indicator (1 = made purchase in this category-period, 0 = no purchase)\n    purchases_in_period = purchases_df.groupby(\n        ['Survey ResponseID', 'Category', 'Period']\n    ).size().reset_index(name='Purchase_Count')\n\n    extensive_data = full_grid.merge(\n        purchases_in_period,\n        on=['Survey ResponseID', 'Category', 'Period'],\n        how='left'\n    )\n\n    # Create binary purchase indicator\n    extensive_data['Has_Purchase'] = (extensive_data['Purchase_Count'] > 0).astype(int)\n    extensive_data.drop('Purchase_Count', axis=1, inplace=True)\n\n    # Merge with engineered features\n    extensive_data = extensive_data.merge(\n        engineered_features_df,\n        on='Survey ResponseID',\n        how='left'\n    )\n\n    #  Multivariate Heterogeneous DiD: Y = α + βX + γPost + δ(X × Post) + ε\n\n        Args:\n            df: Data with 'Period' column ('baseline'/'treatment')\n            features: Feature columns (can asc: bool = False) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Analyze intensive margin (how much behavior changed).\n\n        Uses Multivariate Heterogeneous DiD when sample size is Identify surge and slump categories\n    top_surges = anomaly_index_df.nlargest(10, 'Anomaly_Index')['Category'].tolist()\n    top_slumps = anomaly_index_df.nsmallest(10, 'Anomaly_Index')['Category'].tolist()\n\n    # Identify engineered features\n    engineered_cols = [col for col in intensive_df.columns if col.startswith('ENG_')]\n\n    # Initialize DID analyzer\n    analyzer = DIDAnalyzer()\n\n    # Store results\n    results = {\n        'metadata': {\n            'baseline_start': '01-01-2020',\n            'baseline_end': '02-29-2020',\n            'treatment_start': '03-01-2020',\n            'treatment_end': '03-31-2020',\n            'total_features_analyzed': len(engineered_cols)\n        },\n        'surge_categories': [],\n        'slump_categories': [],\n        'summary': {\n            'surge': {\n                'total_categories': 10,\n                'total_intensive_drivers': 0,\n                'total_extensive_drivers': 0\n            },\n            'slump': {\n                'total_categories': 10,\n                'total_intensive_drivers': 0,\n                'total_extensive_drivers': 0\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    # Get anomaly index for this category\n        anom_idx = anomaly_index_df[anomaly_index_df['Category'] == category]['Anomaly_Index'].values[0]\n\n        # Filter data for this category\n        intensive_cat = intensive_df[intensive_df['Category'] == category].copy()\n        extensive_cat = extensive_df[extensive_df['Category'] == category].copy()\n\n        if len(intensive_cat) == 0 or len(extensive_cat) == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Standardize period names for analysis (lowercase)\n        intensive_cat['Period'] = intensive_cat['Period'].str.lower()\n        extensive_cat['Period'] = extensive_cat['Period'].str.lower()\n\n        # Calculate basic statistics\n        baseline_intensive = intensive_cat[intensive_cat['Period'] == 'baseline']['Total_Spend']\n        treatment_intensive = intensive_cat[intensive_cat['Period'] == 'treatment']['Total_Spend']\n        baseline_extensive = extensive_cat[extensive_cat['Period'] == 'baseline']['Has_Purchase']\n        treatment_extensive = extensive_cat[extensive_cat['Period'] == 'treatment']['Has_Purchase']\n\n        baseline_avg_spend = baseline_intensive.mean() if len(baseline_intensive) > 0 else 0\n        treatment_avg_spend = treatment_intensive.mean() if len(treatment_intensive) > 0 else 0\n        baseline_purchase_rate = baseline_extensive.mean() if len(baseline_extensive) > 0 else 0\n        treatment_purchase_rate = treatment_extensive.mean() if len(treatment_extensive) > 0 else 0\n\n        n_purchasers_baseline = (baseline_extensive > 0).sum()\n        n_purchasers_treatment = (treatment_extensive > 0).sum()\n        n_at_risk = extensive_cat['Survey ResponseID'].nunique()\n\n        # Run DiD analysis for intensive margin\n        intensive_results = []\n        try:\n            for feature in engineered_cols:\n                result = analyzer.analyze_category_outcome(\n                    df=intensive_cat,\n                    features=[feature],\n                    outcome_col='Total_Spend'\n                )\n\n                if result is not None and len(result) > 0:\n                    row = result.iloc[0]\n                    intensive_results.append({\n                        'feature': feature,\n                        'did_estimate': float(row.get('DiD_Estimate', 0)),\n                        'p_value': float(row.get('P_Value', 1.0)),\n                        'method': 'Univariate DiD'\n                    })\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"    Warning: Error in intensive margin analysis: {str(e)}\")\n\n        # Run DiD analysis for extensive margin\n        extensive_results = []\n        try:\n            for feature in engineered_cols:\n                result = analyzer.analyze_category_outcome(\n                    df=extensive_cat,\n                    features=[feature],\n                    outcome_col='Has_Purchase'\n                )\n\n                if result is not None and len(result) > 0:\n                    row = result.iloc[0]\n                    extensive_results.append({\n                        'feature': feature,\n                        'did_estimate': float(row.get('DiD_Estimate', 0)),\n                        'p_value': float(row.get('P_Value', 1.0)),\n                        'method': 'Multivariate Heterogeneous DiD'\n                    })\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"    Warning: Error in extensive margin analysis: {str(e)}\")\n\n        # Keep top 3 for each margin\n        top_intensive = intensive_results[:3]\n        top_extensive = extensive_results[:3]\n\n        category_result = {\n            'category': category,\n            'anomaly_index': float(anom_idx),\n            'baseline_avg_spend': float(baseline_avg_spend),\n            'treatment_avg_spend': float(treatment_avg_spend),\n            'n_purchasers_baseline': int(n_purchasers_baseline),\n            'n_purchasers_treatment': int(n_purchasers_treatment),\n            'baseline_purchase_rate': float(baseline_purchase_rate),\n            'treatment_purchase_rate': float(treatment_purchase_rate),\n            'n_at_risk': int(n_at_risk),\n            'intensive_margin': top_intensive,\n            'extensive_margin': top_extensive\n        }\n\n        results['surge_categories'].append(category_result)\n        results['summary']['surge']['total_intensive_drivers'] += len(top_intensive)\n        results['summary']['surge']['total_extensive_drivers'] += len(top_extensive)\n\n    #
